  queue rows, oldest first, and 44 of them have the review flag on. All 17 tests
  pass and ./verify.sh passes.

    model.py (api/src/assessments/model.py).

  The api container runs uvicorn without --reload, so it only picks up Python
  changes after docker compose restart api. Adding --reload to the Dockerfile
  CMD would fix that, but I haven't changed it.

  I haven't built filtering, paging, issued status, or per-domain bands in the
  rows yet. They can be added with the queue UI or filter task that needs them.

  is actually wrong, instead do the minimal code change needed to pass the
  test

  loaded into every session through CLAUDE.md:

  ▎ When a test fails, change the implementation (minimally) to make it pass,
  ▎ not the test. Only edit a test when the test itself is wrong (asserts the
  ▎ wrong behaviour), and say so.

● Both route groups are now tagged, so the OpenAPI docs group them under
  "health" and "assessments". The tag goes on each feature's APIRouter, so any
  route added to that router later gets it automatically:

  - assessments/routes.py (api/src/assessments/routes.py):
    APIRouter(tags=["assessments"])
  - health/routes.py (api/src/health/routes.py): APIRouter(tags=["health"])

  both paths' tags in the generated OpenAPI schema. It failed before the change,
  and now all 18 tests pass.

  before checking the grouping at http://localhost:8000/docs.

● I moved db.py into the new src/middleware/ folder, and everything works again:
  all 18 tests pass and ./verify.sh passes.

  - Move: api/src/utils/{__init__,db}.py became api/src/middleware/ via git mv,
    so git records it as a rename.
  - utils/ deleted: it held only db.py, so I removed it rather than leave an
    empty folder. Its cache files belonged to root inside the container, so I
    deleted them through the container.
  - Imports: main.py (api/src/main.py) and routes.py
    (api/src/assessments/routes.py) now import src.middleware.db.
  - Docs: the layout in api/README.md now lists middleware/, and I corrected its
    outdated "no HTTP routes yet" note. I added an entry to decisions.md
    (agent/decisions.md) and updated my memory note on the project layout.

  My first attempt stopped partway through, which left the api crashing on the
  old import for a moment. It's fixed now.

  In FastAPI, "middleware" usually means per-request hooks like the CORS
  middleware in main.py. Naming a folder of connection helpers middleware/ may
  confuse someone who knows FastAPI; integrations/ or clients/ would avoid that.
  It's your call, and I've left it as middleware/ for now.

  Your changes are staged in git but not committed.

❯ what's next on the list

● From the plan we agreed at the start (queue → filter → open → issue), the
  queue endpoint is done. What's left:

  1. Filtering the queue. Add query parameters to GET /assessments, such as
     review_flag and clinician_id, done in SQL as we decided. Filtering by
     issued/unissued has to wait for item 3.
  2. Opening one assessment. Add GET /assessments/{id} returning the scores,
     bands, the young person's age at assessment, and the summary. Only the
     client fields the page actually shows would be sent. This is also the first
     place to record a view in the audit log.
  3. Issuing a report. Add an issued status to the table and an endpoint to
     issue a report. After that, the report can't be changed, except for summary
     corrections.
  4. Summary corrections after issue. This needs building from scratch, and each
     correction gets an audit entry.
  5. Audit log table. This records who viewed what and what changed. Items 2–4
     all write to it, so it should come before them or together with item 2.
  6. Mock clinician identity. A clinician picked from a fixed list, so the audit
     entries have a "who". The api needs this before item 5 means anything.
  7. Web app. The queue page, then the detail page and the issue action. Per
     testing.md, vitest has to go in with the first web change.

  Your current work (queue endpoint, OpenAPI tags, the clients/ rename) is
  staged but not committed, so it's worth committing first.

  I'd start with the web queue page next, including vitest. The queue endpoint
  already exists, so this gives you a working path from the database to the
  browser early and shows up any CORS or data problems now rather than at the
  end. If you'd rather keep going on the api, filtering (item 1) is the smallest
  next step.

❯ I want to build the UI as I go along too so lets start on displaying the
  queue page. Show me what you're thinking first for me to review

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Here's what I'm proposing for the queue page. Nothing is written yet.

  What it shows

  Assessment Review
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Review queue · 100 assessments · 44 need review

   Assessment   Assessed       Clinician   Status
   a-00052      3 Apr 2025     c-008       ⚑ Needs review
   a-00017      9 Apr 2025     c-003       –
   a-00081      14 Apr 2025    c-001       ⚑ Needs review
   ...

  - It's a plain table showing the four fields GET /assessments already returns,
    in the same order (oldest first). Sorting stays on the api.
  - Flag: a flagged row shows a "⚑ Needs review" label, written out in words
    rather than shown only as a colour, so it doesn't depend on seeing colour.
  - Dates: shown in UK format ("3 Apr 2025"), since this is an NHS setting.
  - Loading, error and empty states: "Loading…", "Couldn't load the queue" with
    a Retry button, and "No assessments".
  - Not included yet: clicking a row (the detail page doesn't exist), filters
    (the api doesn't support them), and paging (100 rows is fine).

  Files

  web/src/
    App.tsx                         # renders <QueuePage />; the health-check
  text goes
    assessments/
      api.ts                        # QueueItem type + fetchQueue()
      QueuePage.tsx                 # fetch in useEffect, loading / error /
  table states
      QueuePage.test.tsx
    index.css                       # small plain stylesheet, no styling library

  This mirrors the api's feature folders. There's no router yet; I'd add one
  with the detail page, when there's a second page to route to.

  Testing (the first web task, so vitest goes in now)

  - Dev dependencies: vitest, jsdom, @testing-library/react. All three are well
    maintained; jsdom is the biggest.
  - Install: through the container (docker compose run web npm install -D ...),
    not on the host, to avoid the rollup lockfile bug already recorded in
    decisions.md.
  - Test command: npm test runs vitest run. I'd add it to verify.sh as its own
    check alongside api pytest, and record the command in testing.md.
  - Tests, written first: fetch is replaced with a fake in the tests, so they
    don't need the api running.
    a. Rows appear in the order the api returns them.
    b. Flagged rows show "Needs review" and unflagged rows don't.
    c. The count line reads "100 assessments · 44 need review" (with small
       numbers in the test).
    d. When the api returns an error, the error message and a Retry button
       appear, and Retry fetches again.

  Questions for you

  1. Flag label: "Needs review" OK, or something else ("Flagged", "Check before
     issuing")?

  - Loading: about 8 grey placeholder rows (a skeleton) with a shimmer. The
    shimmer is turned off when the user's system asks for reduced motion.
  - Error: "Couldn't load the queue" plus a Retry button in the site's
    purple-gradient button style.
  - Empty: "No assessments".
  - Rows: no hover or click behaviour until the detail page exists.
  - Colours: Neurotype's colours defined once as CSS variables, with a dark
    palette used when the OS is in dark mode. The logo swaps from black to white
    in dark mode.
  - Font: Open Sans from a Google Fonts <link>.
  - Logo files: the black and white SVGs saved into web/public/.

  API change (first, test-first)

  Flagged-first ordering belongs in the api, so the query becomes ORDER BY
  review_flag DESC, assessed_at. The existing oldest-first test is wrong under
  the new rule, so it would be one of the rare correct test edits. I'll change
  it to assert "flagged first, then oldest" before touching the query.

  Web structure

  web/src/
    store.ts                    # configureStore with the RTK Query api
    assessments/
      assessmentsApi.ts         # createApi: getQueue → GET /assessments,
  QueueItem type
      QueuePage.tsx             # uses useGetQueueQuery; loading / error / table
      QueuePage.module.css
      QueuePage.test.tsx
    Header.tsx + Header.module.css
    theme.css                   # colour variables (light + dark), fonts, base
  styles
    App.tsx                     # Header + QueuePage
    main.tsx                    # wraps the app in the Redux <Provider>

  Dependencies (installed through the container, per decisions.md)

  - Runtime: @reduxjs/toolkit, react-redux
  - Dev: vitest, jsdom, @testing-library/react
  - npm test runs vitest run, and I'll add it to verify.sh as its own check.

  Tests (written first; fetch is faked, so the api doesn't need to run)

  1. Loading shows the placeholder rows.
  2. Rows show up in the order the api returns them.
  3. Flagged rows show "Needs review"; unflagged rows don't.
  4. The count line reads "3 assessments · 2 need review" (small fixture
     numbers).
  5. An api error shows the message; Retry fetches again and then shows the
     rows.

  Rule and doc updates

  - react.md: "API calls go through RTK Query; state shared between components

  - decisions.md: an entry for the RTK Query + Redux choice and the design
    source.

● I've added it to testing.md (agent/rules/testing.md):

  ▎ Test data goes in pytest fixtures (or the web runner's equivalent), not
  ▎ built inline in the test body. The test body only loads or uses the data,
  ▎ acts, and asserts.

  Some existing tests already break the new rule:
  - test_service.py: almost every test builds its Domain/Item objects inline.
  - test_store.py: RECORD is a module-level constant, and the upsert test builds
    {**RECORD, "clinician_id": "c-099"} inline.
  - test_routes.py: test_queue_rows_carry_review_fields_and_no_client_pii uses
    RECORD directly.

  leave them until those files are next touched. Otherwise, once you've reviewed
  this, we're ready for step 2 (web dependencies and test setup).

● I'll switch to Vitest's .extend(name, fn) builder form, which avoids the

❯ I prefer to have a components folder to keep my reusable components, styles
  folder to keep common styles, services folder to kee API logic (rtk calls to
  endpoints). The pages folder will be for each url and I like to colocate
  files so separate out interfaces, types into their own files ending with
  .interface.ts, .type.ts. Unit test files are also colocated with its file and
  end with test.ts

  features folder contains the redux slices, and a tests folder in the same
  level as the src folder to keep end to end tests

● I'll restructure what exists now and record the layout as a rule. Folders that
  would be empty (components/, styles/, pages/, features/, tests/) get created
  when their first file lands, since git doesn't keep empty folders anyway.

  - conventions.md (agent/rules/conventions.md): a new "web layout" section with
    the folder map, the naming rule for colocated files, and "create a folder
    when its first file lands".
  - typescript.md (agent/rules/typescript.md): types go in <name>.type.ts and
    interfaces in <name>.interface.ts, next to the file that owns them. This
    replaces the old rule, which kept types inline until a second module needed
    them.
  - react.md (agent/rules/react.md):
    - API calls go in services/, shared state in features/ slices, and
      single-component state in useState. This replaces the old "no state
      library" rule.
    - Page-specific parts stay in the page's folder until a second page uses
      them.
  - testing.md (agent/rules/testing.md): unit tests sit next to the file they
    cover, and end-to-end tests go in web/tests/.

  Choices I made where your description didn't say; tell me if any are wrong
  1. Props types: also go in their own file, e.g. QueuePage.type.ts, not inline
     in the component.
  2. File naming: components are PascalCase (QueuePage.tsx) and everything else
     is camelCase (assessmentsApi.ts, queueItem.type.ts).
  3. Shared types: QueueItem stays in services/ next to the API that returns it,
     and pages import it from there.
  4. Store: store.ts stays at src/store.ts; features/ holds only slices.
  5. End-to-end tests: no runner chosen yet. When you want e2e tests, Playwright
     is the usual pick.

  ⎿  Updated agent/rules/conventions.md (+4 -0)
      27  
      28  - Colocate: a file's types (`.type.ts`), interfaces (`.interfac
          e.ts`), styles
      29    (`.module.css`) and unit tests (`.test.ts` / `.test.tsx`) sit
           next to it.
      30 +- A module with colocated files (its test, types, styles) gets 
         +its own folder named after it.                                 
      31 +  The module is a default export, and the folder's `index.ts` r
         +e-exports it                                                   
      32 +  (`export default getApiUrl`), so callers import the folder: `
         +import getApiUrl from                                          
      33 +  '../utils/getApiUrl'`. Example: `utils/getApiUrl/{getApiUrl.t
         +s, getApiUrl.test.ts, index.ts}`.                              
      34  - Create a folder when its first file lands. Don't add empty fo
          lders ahead of time.
      35  
      36  Language-specific conventions: [python.md](python.md), [typescr
          ipt.md](typescript.md),

● I've added it to conventions.md (agent/rules/conventions.md):

  ▎ A module with colocated files (its test, types, styles) gets its own folder
  ▎ named after it. The module is a default export, and the folder's index.ts
  ▎ re-exports it, so callers import the folder: import getApiUrl from
  ▎ '../utils/getApiUrl'.

  The step 4 components will follow it: components/Header/ and pages/QueuePage/
  each get an index.ts.

  services/assessments/ doesn't fit this rule cleanly. assessmentsApi.ts exports
  two named things (assessmentsApi and the useGetQueueQuery hook), and the
  folder will get more endpoints over time. My suggestion is an index.ts there
  that re-exports both by name, so callers write import { useGetQueueQuery }
  from '../../services/assessments'. Should I add that, or leave services as
  they are?

      14 +  `types/queueItem.type.ts`) go in `src/types/`. Types only one
         + module uses, like component                                   
      15 +  props (`QueuePage.type.ts`), sit next to that module.        
      16  - No non-null assertions (`!`) to silence the compiler — handle
           the `undefined`/`null` case or
      17    narrow properly.

  - Aliases: there's one for every top-level src/ folder: components/,
    features/, pages/, services/, styles/, types/ and utils/.

    tsconfig.json (web/tsconfig.json) and resolve.alias in vite.config.ts

    says a new top-level folder needs adding to both config files.

  Still relative: store.ts is a file at the root of src/, not a folder, so the
  test's import { makeStore } from '../../store' is unchanged. I can add a store
  alias too, if you like.

  One caveat: the bare names can clash with npm packages that have the same
  name. There are real packages called utils, types and components. Nothing
  installed today uses those names. If a dependency ever does, the fix is a
  prefix such as @/types/....

  What's on the page
  - Header: the Neurotype logo (the white one in dark mode), a divider, and
    "Assessment Review".

    review" with "review" in the gradient, and the line "100 assessments · 44
    need review".

    UK time), Clinician and Status. Flagged rows show an indigo "Needs review"
    label and come first, as the api now orders them.
  - Loading: 8 pulsing placeholder rows, plus hidden "Loading assessments…" text
    for screen readers. The pulsing stops when the OS asks for reduced motion.
  - Error: "Couldn't load the queue." with a gradient Retry button.
  - Empty: "No assessments".

● I've added a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility rule, and the logo now scales with the
  screen instead of being a fixed size. All checks pass: the build type-checks,
  and ./verify.sh passes (api and web tests, ESLint, Prettier). I haven't
  checked the page in a browser, so look at it at narrow widths and at 200%
  zoom.

  The rule

  agent/rules/accessibility.md is new and linked from CLAUDE.md, so every
  session loads it. Each point cites its WCAG criterion:
  - Sizes in rem/em/clamp(), with no fixed px except 1–2px borders and focus
    outlines. The page must work at 200% zoom and at 320px wide.
  - Text contrast of at least 4.5:1, and 3:1 for focus rings and control edges,
    checked in both light and dark palettes.
  - Status is never shown by colour alone.
  - Everything works from the keyboard, with a visible focus ring.
  - Semantic HTML: one <h1>, table headers, alt text.
  - Loading and error messages are announced to screen readers (role="status" /
    role="alert").
  - Animation stops when the OS asks for reduced motion.
  - Tests find elements by role and accessible name.

  The logo
  - The logo height is clamp(1.5rem, 1rem + 2vw, 2.25rem) in Header.module.css
    (web/src/components/Header/Header.module.css). It grows with screen width
    between 24px and 36px, and also grows with the user's font-size setting.
  - The divider next to it now scales with the title text.
  - The header can wrap onto two lines when zoomed in, instead of running off
    the side of the screen.

  Brought into line with the rule
  - Stylesheets: every size in the three stylesheets is now in rem, except
    hairline borders, focus outlines and the 1px screen-reader-only trick.
  - Heading font size: used a pure 5vw middle value, which doesn't grow when the
    user zooms text only. It's now 1.25rem + 2.5vw, so it responds to both
    screen width and zoom.
  - Contrast: I measured every existing colour pair and all meet AA already. The
    lowest is the "Review queue" label at 4.78:1 (the minimum is 4.5:1).

  The table still scrolls sideways on very narrow screens. WCAG allows this for
  data tables, and the rule says so.

  ake-home-exercise/web/src/pages/QueuePage/QueuePage.module.scss in the IDE.
  This may or may not be related to the current task.</ide_opened_file>

❯ why add .module.scss, I present just QueuePage.scss

● It's the suffix that makes Vite treat the file as a CSS Module. Vite only
  scopes class names for files named *.module.scss. A plain QueuePage.scss gets
  imported as a global stylesheet, and that changes three things:

  1. Class names clash. QueuePage.module.scss defines .table, .card, .page and
     .message. As modules they compile to unique names like _table_105a2_56, so
     another page can have its own .table safely. As a plain .scss they're
     global, and the first other component that uses .card or .table would
     silently pick up these styles, or override them.
  2. The import changes. Today it's import styles from './QueuePage.module.scss'
     and className={styles.table}. A plain .scss is imported just for its side
     effect (import './QueuePage.scss'), and you write className="table" strings
     instead.
  3. Typos go unnoticed. With a plain file, a misspelled class name silently
     doesn't match anything.

  As far as I know, Vite has no setting to make plain .scss files behave as
  modules; it decides by the .module. in the name. You also picked CSS Modules
  when I asked about styling earlier, which is why I named them that way.

  If the .module suffix is what bothers you, the alternative is plain
  QueuePage.scss with unique class names you manage yourself, usually BEM style
  (.queue-page__table, .queue-page__flag--active). It works, but staying
  clash-free becomes a naming discipline instead of something the build
  guarantees.

  I'd keep .module.scss. Want me to keep it, or switch to plain .scss with BEM
  names?

❯ what's the pros and cons for each

● Here's how the two compare:

  Clash safety
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): Guaranteed by the build: .table becomes
  _table_105a2_56, unique to this file
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): Depends on everyone following the naming
  convention; one lazy .card leaks everywhere
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Class names you write
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): Short and local (.table, .flag)
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): Long and prefixed (.queue-page__table,
  .queue-page__flag)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Usage in JSX
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): className={styles.table}
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): className="queue-page__table"
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Typos
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): Show up at runtime as a missing class
  (TypeScript doesn't type the class names out of the box)
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): Silently match nothing
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Dead styles
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): Easy to spot: search for styles.x
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): Harder, because strings can be built
  dynamically
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Styling a child or third-party element
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): Needs :global(.some-class) to reach names

  you don't own
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): Straightforward, since everything is
  global
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Class names in DevTools
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): Hashed (_table_105a2_56), though the
  original name is still readable in it
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): Readable exactly as written
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Multiple classes / conditions
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): className={`${styles.flag}
  ${styles.active}`}, or a helper like clsx
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): className="flag flag--active"
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Tests
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): Vitest doesn't process CSS by default, so

  class names are opaque (fine, since the tests query by role)
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): Same
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File name
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): Needs .module in the name
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): Plain .scss
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Familiarity
  QueuePage.module.scss (CSS Modules): Standard in Vite, Next.js and Create
  React
  App projects
  QueuePage.scss (global, BEM names): Classic approach, common in older and
  non-React codebases

  In short:
  - CSS Modules: the build guarantees components can't break each other's
    styles. You pay in hashed names, the .module suffix and styles.x syntax.
  - Global BEM: readable names and simple JSX. You depend on everyone following
    the naming convention, and a clash shows up as a visual bug somewhere
    unrelated.

  For this project I'd still pick CSS Modules. There will be several pages
  (queue, detail, audit) that each want generic names like .table, .card and
  .message, and nothing would stop those clashing except discipline. Tell me
  which you want and I'll record it in conventions.md either way.

● Here's the plan for filtering. Nothing is written yet. I've split it into
  steps that you can review one at a time.

  API

  Step 1: scores in the queue rows
  - Each row gains domain_scores, the data behind the band columns. It's already
    stored per assessment, so no scoring happens per request:
    {"social_communication": {"percentage": 62.5, "band": "moderate"}, …}
  - A domain with no completed items has null for both values.

  Step 2: filter parameters on GET /assessments, all optional and combinable,
  all done in SQL

  Parameter: review_flag=true|false
  Meaning: flagged or not
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Parameter: clinician_id=c-008
  Meaning: exact match
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Parameter: assessed_from=2025-04-01, assessed_to=2025-04-30
  Meaning: inclusive, by UK calendar day
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Parameter: search=0005
  Meaning: assessment ID contains, case-insensitive; % and _ are escaped
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Parameter: score_domain=executive_function&score_min=55&score_max=84
  Meaning: percentage range in one domain, inclusive; domains that weren't
  assessed never match

  - Bad input returns a 422 error. That covers an unknown domain, min above max,
    values outside 0–100, a "from" date after the "to" date, or
    score_min/score_max without score_domain.
  - There's no database index on the score column yet. I'll add one if query
    plans show it's needed.

  Step 3: GET /clinicians
  - Returns the distinct clinician IDs, sorted: ["c-001", "c-002", …].
  - It lives in its own src/clinicians/ feature folder. It's also the start of
    the mock clinician list we'll need later.

  Web

  Step 4: band columns
  - Five short columns (Social, Sensory, Executive, Emotional, Motor), each with
    a band label: Minimal, Mild, Moderate or Substantial. The word is always
    written out, with colour only as a second signal, and a domain that wasn't
    assessed shows "Not assessed".
  - The percentage appears as a tooltip and in screen-reader text.

  Step 5: collapsible filter panel, with filters kept in the URL

   ● REVIEW QUEUE
   Assessments awaiting review
   44 of 100 assessments · 44 need review          [ Filters (2) ▾ ]
   ╭──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
   │ Search ID [______]   Review [All ▾]   Clinician [All ▾]      │
   │ Assessed from [date]  to [date]                              │
   │ Score  domain [Executive function ▾]  from [55]% to [84]%    │
   │                                   [Clear]  [Apply filters]   │
   ╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
   (table)

  - The button shows how many filters are active and uses aria-expanded, so
    screen readers know whether the panel is open.
  - The panel is a form of native controls, and nothing changes until you press
    Apply. That avoids refetching on every keystroke and makes a clear moment to
    update the URL.
  - Filters live in the URL (/?review_flag=true&clinician_id=c-008), so a reload
    keeps them and the back button works.
  - There's no router yet. The page reads the URL, pushes a new URL on Apply,
    and listens for the back button. A router comes in with the detail page.
  - The panel starts open when the URL already has filters in it.
  - GET /clinicians gets its own endpoint in services/clinicians/.

  Each step is written test-first, and the full checks run at the end of each
  one.

  Two defaults to confirm or change:
  1. Apply button, rather than filtering as soon as a control changes.
  2. No router yet, rather than adding react-router now for the URL handling.

  Shall I start step 1?

  Tests, written first (test_routes.py (api/tests/assessments/test_routes.py))
  - Data: a filterable_records fixture with 4 records. Between them they cover:
    flagged and unflagged, two clinicians, IDs that do and don't match "a-0005",
    scores of 95%, 25% and 60%, a domain with no score, and 23:30 UTC on 30
    April, which is 1 May in the UK.
  - Filters that should work: 13 cases, covering each filter alone, both ends of
    the ranges, the UK-day boundary, a % search that must not act as a
    wildcard, and two filters combined.
  - Input that should be rejected (422):
    - review_flag=maybe
    - a from-date after the to-date
    - an unknown domain
    - scores below 0 or above 100
    - a minimum above the maximum
    - a score range with no domain

  Code
  - model.py (api/src/assessments/model.py):
    - A DomainName type lists the five domains.
    - A QueueFilters pydantic model holds the parameters, with a validator for
      the rules that involve two fields at once.
    - It's the first pydantic model in model.py, which was dataclasses-only
      until now. FastAPI needs pydantic to read the query string into a model,
      so this is deliberate.
  - store.py (api/src/assessments/store.py):
    - _queue_conditions builds the SQL WHERE clause from the filters. Every
      value goes in as a query parameter, never pasted into the SQL.
    - Dates are compared as UK calendar days.
    - The search escapes %, _ and \ so they match literally.
    - A score filter never matches a domain that wasn't assessed.
  - routes.py (api/src/assessments/routes.py): the route takes
    Annotated[QueueFilters, Query()].

  Skipped: there's no index on the score expression. It's worth adding if query
  plans on the real data volume show full-table scans.

❯ rule of thumb for the api file structure: models describe data, service.py
  makes decisions, store.py handles persistence and routes.py handles HTTP. If
  a function needs neither a database connection nor a request, it goes in
  service.py. based on this update the folders

● The clear-cut moves are done and nothing changed in behaviour: the startup
  load still reports 100 assessments with the same scores, all 46 api tests
  pass, and ./verify.sh passes.

  Moved
  - parse_assessment (was _parse_assessment) moved from store.py into service.py
    (api/src/assessments/service.py). It turns a line of the data file into an
    Assessment, which is a decision and needs no database.
  - domain_scores (was _domain_scores) moved from store.py into service.py. It
    now returns DomainScore dataclasses instead of plain dicts. store.py
    converts them to JSON only when it writes the row.
  - Band moved from service.py into model.py (api/src/assessments/model.py),
    since it describes data. Only the import line in test_service.py changed;
    the tests themselves didn't.

  Rule recorded: python.md (agent/rules/python.md) now describes what each file
  is for and includes your rule of thumb. The layout in api/README.md matches.

  Three helpers in store.py (api/src/assessments/store.py) need neither a
  database connection nor a request, so taken literally the rule sends them to
  service.py. But what they produce is SQL or database columns:
  - _row_for(assessment): maps an Assessment to the table's column names.
  - _queue_conditions(filters): builds the SQL WHERE clauses from the filters.
  - _like_pattern(text): escapes %, _ and \ for SQL ILIKE.

  Moving them would put SQL text into service.py, which clashes with "store
  handles persistence". I'd keep them in store.py and refine the rule to "pure
  logic goes in service.py; anything that shapes SQL or table columns stays in
  store.py, even if it doesn't take a connection." Should I refine the rule that
  way, or follow it literally and move them?

❯ sounds good

❯ use prettier tooltips for the hover over and have a lighter shading over the
  hovered over row

● Two things change: the native tooltips get replaced with styled ones, and
  table rows get a light tint on hover. A plain CSS tooltip isn't enough here,
  for two reasons:
  - Accessibility (WCAG 1.4.13): content that appears on hover must be
    dismissible with Esc, stay open while the pointer is over it, and not vanish
    on its own. Esc needs a little JavaScript.
  - Clipping: the table's card scrolls sideways, and that cuts off anything
    positioned outside it. A tooltip positioned relative to the viewport isn't
    cut off.

  So this is a small Tooltip component with no new dependency. It lives in
  pages/QueuePage/Tooltip/ because only the queue page uses it, per the rule
  about page-specific parts. Test first:

● Bash(cd /home/sbenny1230/GitHub/neurotype-take-home-exercise/web && mkdir -p
      src/pages/QueuePage/Tooltip && cat >
      src/pages/QueuePage/Tooltip/Tooltip.test.tsx <<'EOF…)
  ⎿  Created web/src/pages/QueuePage/Tooltip/Tooltip.test.tsx (+41 -0)

● The styled tooltips and the row hover tint are in, and all checks pass: 14 web
  tests, the build and ./verify.sh. I haven't seen them in a browser, so the
  look and placement are worth a hover-through.

  Tooltips (pages/QueuePage/Tooltip/ (web/src/pages/QueuePage/Tooltip/))
  - Look: a navy bubble with white text and a small arrow (inverted in dark
    mode), sitting just above what you hover.
  - Where: band cells show the percentage ("52.8%"). Domain headers show the
    full name ("Emotional regulation"); I removed the <abbr> so you don't also
    get the browser's own tooltip.
  - WCAG 1.4.13 behaviour:
    - Esc dismisses it.
    - It stays open while you move the pointer onto it.
    - It stays until the pointer leaves.
    - It closes on scroll so it can't drift away from its cell.
  - Clipping: it's positioned against the screen rather than the table, so the
    card's sideways scroll can't cut it off.
  - Tests, written first: hidden until hovered, shown on hover, hidden when the
    pointer leaves, and dismissed by Esc.

  Row hover: a very light indigo tint (4% in light mode, 7% in dark) that fades
  in, with no fade when reduced motion is on. The tooltip colours are 16:1
  (light) and 15:1 (dark).

  Two accessibility gaps, both smaller than before:
  1. Keyboard users can't open the tooltips. Making every band cell focusable
     would add about 500 tab stops. Screen readers still get each percentage
     from the cell's hidden text. Sighted keyboard users will get the
     percentages once rows link to the detail page.
  2. Screen readers lost the full domain names in the headers. They now hear the
     short header ("Social") without "Social communication". If you want it
     back, I can add hidden text; the header test would then need its expected
     text updated.

  Once you've reviewed this, step 5 is the filter panel.

  One shared RTK Query instance

    getQueue to it. The filters are sent as query parameters, and none are sent
    when there are no filters.

    (web/src/services/clinicians/cliniciansApi.ts) (new): getClinicians calls
    GET /clinicians.

  New type: types/queueFilters.type.ts (web/src/types/queueFilters.type.ts)
  holds the filter names, with all values as strings. That mirrors the URL, so
  the filters go from address bar to api request without conversion. The api
  validates and converts them.

● Step 5b: the filter panel and keeping the filters in the URL. Here's how it
  splits up, all inside pages/QueuePage/ since only this page uses it:
  - useUrlFilters/: a hook that reads the filters from the URL, pushes a new URL
    when filters are applied, and follows the back button.
  - FilterPanel/: the collapsible form. It fetches the clinician list only once
    it's opened, so the page itself doesn't make an extra request on load.

    panel's domain dropdown.

● Step 5b is done, and filtering works end to end. All checks pass: 31 web
  tests, the type-checking build, the api tests, ESLint, Prettier and
  ./verify.sh. I haven't used the panel in a browser, so opening it, applying a
  couple of filters and pressing the back button is the check left for you.

  How it behaves
  - The toggle: a "Filters ▾" button under the heading, which reads "Filters (2
    active)" when filters are on. It opens a card holding the form, and starts
    open if the URL already has filters.
  - Fields:
    - Assessment ID
    - Review status (Any / Needs review / No review needed)
    - Clinician (list loaded from GET /clinicians when the panel opens)
    - Assessed from and to (the browser's date pickers)
    - Score domain, plus score from and to in %
  - Apply filters: updates the URL (e.g. ?review_flag=true&clinician_id=c-008),
    refetches the queue and dims the table while it loads. Reload keeps the
    filters, and back and forward step through earlier filter sets.
  - Clear filters: removes them all.
  - Invalid input: a plain-English message appears (announced to screen
    readers), and nothing is sent to the api. That covers a score range without
    a domain, "from" above "to", and dates the wrong way round.
  - Accessibility:
    - The toggle uses aria-expanded.
    - Every field has a label.
    - Borders on inputs and buttons meet the 3:1 contrast rule in both themes,
      using a new --color-control-border token.
    - The fields reflow to one column at 320px.
    - The chevron animation respects reduced motion.

  Files (all page-specific, so all in pages/QueuePage/
  (web/src/pages/QueuePage/))
  - useUrlFilters/ (web/src/pages/QueuePage/useUrlFilters/useUrlFilters.ts):

    pushes a new history entry on Apply, and follows back and forward.
  - FilterPanel/ (web/src/pages/QueuePage/FilterPanel/FilterPanel.tsx):
    - The toggle, plus an inner FilterForm that's only mounted while open, so
      the clinician list isn't fetched until needed.
    - The form rebuilds when the applied filters change, so back and forward
      refresh the field values too.
  - domains.ts (web/src/pages/QueuePage/domains.ts): the five domains' short and
    full names, now shared by the table columns and the domain dropdown.
  - styles/_buttons.scss (web/src/styles/_buttons.scss): primary and secondary
    button mixins. The existing Retry button now uses the primary one.

  Tests, written first (15 new)
  - URL hook (4): reading the URL, applying (including the new history entry),

  - Panel (9): starting closed, toggling, starting open with a count and
    pre-filled values, the clinician list, applying with empty fields left out,
    clearing, and the three validation messages.
  - Page (2): it requests the queue with the URL's filters, and Apply refetches

● I've fixed the missing editor types. Your code was fine; VS Code just couldn't
  see any installed packages. To clear the red squiggles, reload the editor's
  TypeScript checker: Command Palette → TypeScript: Restart TS Server (or reload
  the window).

  Cause: the container installs packages into its own storage, which is hidden
  from your machine, so web/node_modules on the host was an empty folder. The
  container build always type-checked fine; only the editor was missing the
  types.

  Fix: I ran npm ci in web/ on the host.
  - npm ci installs exactly what package-lock.json lists and doesn't rewrite it;
    I checked that the lockfile is byte-for-byte unchanged. Plain npm install
    is what caused the earlier lockfile bug.
  - react/jsx-runtime and its types now exist on the host, and tsc type-checks
    cleanly there.

  (agent/decisions.md), so a fresh clone knows to run npm ci for the editor.

  Harmless warning during install: two of jsdom's helper packages want Node
  24.15 or newer, and your host has 24.14.1. It only matters if you run the
  tests on the host rather than in the container. Updating Node through nvm (nvm
  install 24) would silence it.
